Enterprise IT Service & Asset Operations Assistant
Project Overview
The Enterprise IT Service & Asset Operations Assistant is an AI-powered application designed to help employees with common IT service and asset-related queries..
hi surya
The application uses a locally running Large Language Model (LLM) through Ollama and a tool-based AI agent to retrieve information, execute predefined operations, and provide responses to user questions.

It combines Large Language Models, tool calling, Retrieval-Augmented Generation (RAG), embeddings, and a web-based chat interface to provide an interactive IT support experience.
Key Objectives
- Retrieve employee IT asset information.
- Check IT service ticket details and status.
- Identify IT support teams and their responsibilities.
- Answer IT policy and troubleshooting questions using an internal knowledge guide.
- Handle multi-step questions by executing multiple tools.
- Prepare incident report drafts for human review.
- Display tool execution activity to improve transparency.
Technology Stack
The project is developed using the following technologies:
Technology	Purpose
Python	Backend application development
FastAPI	Building REST API endpoints
Ollama	Running Large Language Models locally
Qwen 2.5 (3B)	Generating AI responses and handling tool calls
Nomic Embed Text	Generating embeddings for knowledge retrieval
Requests	Communicating with the Ollama API
python-dotenv	Managing environment variables
HTML	Building the chat interface
CSS	Styling the web application
JavaScript	Handling frontend interactions and API communication
Uvicorn	Running the FastAPI application


AI and Retrieval Components
- LLM: Qwen 2.5 (3B), used for natural language understanding and response generation.
- Embedding Model: Nomic Embed Text, used to convert IT guide content and user queries into numerical vectors.
- RAG: Retrieves relevant sections from the internal IT operations guide before generating policy-related answers.
- Tool Calling: Allows the AI agent to invoke predefined Python functions to retrieve assets, tickets, support teams, and prepare incident drafts.
Project Architecture
The application follows a modular architecture in which the frontend, API layer, AI agent, tools, knowledge retrieval system, and local LLM communicate with each other.
High-Level Architecture
                 USER
                   |
                   v
          Web Chat Interface
          (HTML, CSS, JavaScript)
                   |
                   v
             FastAPI Backend
                   |
                   v
               AI Agent
                   |
          +--------+--------+
          |                 |
          v                 v
     Tool Execution    Knowledge Retrieval
          |                 |
          v                 v
      Python Tools     IT Operations Guide
          |                 |
          +--------+--------+
                   |
                   v
              Ollama Client
                   |
                   v
              Ollama Server
                   |
             +-----+------+
             |            |
             v            v
          Qwen 2.5    Nomic Embed
             |            |
             v            v
        AI Response    Embeddings
Main Application Modules
Module	Responsibility
app/config.py	Loads application configuration and environment variables
app/ollama_client.py	Communicates with the locally running Ollama API
app/tools.py	Implements asset, ticket, support-team, and incident-draft operations
app/knowledge.py	Loads IT guide sections, generates embeddings, and performs similarity-based retrieval
app/agent.py	Manages the AI agent, system prompt, tool execution, and multi-step workflows
app/main.py	Defines FastAPI endpoints and serves the frontend
run.py	Starts the Uvicorn server


Supporting Directories
Directory	Purpose
data/	Contains the internal IT operations knowledge guide
static/	Contains HTML, CSS, and JavaScript frontend files
tests/	Contains standalone test scripts and automated unit tests

Installation and Setup
Follow these steps to run the application locally.
Prerequisites
Make sure the following software is installed:
- Python 3.10 or later
- Ollama
- Git (optional)
Step 1: Clone or Download the Project
Download the project source code and navigate to the project directory.
Step 2: Create a Virtual Environment
python -m venv .venv
Step 3: Activate the Virtual Environment
.venv\Scripts\Activate.ps1
Step 4: Install Dependencies
pip install -r requirements.txt
Step 5: Configure Environment Variables
Copy .env.example to .env and configure the following values:
OLLAMA_BASE_URL=http://localhost:11434
CHAT_MODEL=qwen2.5:3b
EMBED_MODEL=nomic-embed-text
MAX_AGENT_STEPS=4
Step 6: Download Ollama Models
Run the following commands:
ollama pull qwen2.5:3b
ollama pull nomic-embed-text
Step 7: Start Ollama
Make sure the Ollama application or service is running locally.
Step 8: Start the Application
From the project root directory, execute:
python run.py
The FastAPI application will start at:
http://127.0.0.1:8000
Open the URL in your browser to access the IT Service Assistant.
API Documentation
The application uses FastAPI to expose REST API endpoints for interacting with the IT Service Assistant.
Base URL
http://127.0.0.1:8000
Available Endpoints
Method	Endpoint	Description
GET	/	Serves the web-based chat interface
GET	/health	Checks whether the API is responding
POST	/chat	Sends a question to the AI agent
POST	/api/chat	Sends a question and returns the answer with tool execution activity


1. Health Check
Endpoint: GET /health

Checks whether the FastAPI application is running.

Example response:
{
    "status": "healthy"
}
2. Chat Endpoint
Endpoint: POST /chat

Sends a user question to the AI agent and returns its response.

Example request:
{
    "message": "Who is assigned asset AST-1001?"
}
Example response:
{
    "response": "Asset AST-1001 is assigned to Nisha Rao."
}
3. Chat Endpoint with Activity Trace
Endpoint: POST /api/chat

This endpoint returns the AI-generated answer along with the tools executed by the agent.

Example request:
{
    "message": "Check ticket INC-2041 and explain the escalation process."
}
Example response structure:
{
    "answer": "Ticket INC-2041 is currently in progress...",
    "trace": [
        {
            "tool": "get_ticket",
            "arguments": {
                "ticket_id": "INC-2041"
            },
            "result": {
                "found": true
            }
        },
        {
            "tool": "search_it_guide",
            "arguments": {
                "question": "Explain the ticket escalation process..."
            },
            "result": {
                "found": true
            }
        }
    ]
}
The example above illustrates the response structure. Actual tool results contain additional information.
Interactive API Documentation
FastAPI automatically provides interactive API documentation through Swagger UI.

Open the following URL after starting the application:
http://127.0.0.1:8000/docs
Swagger UI allows developers to:
- View available API endpoints.
- Inspect request and response schemas.
- Execute API requests.
- Review API responses.
Core AI Capabilities
The Enterprise IT Service Assistant combines several AI concepts to provide intelligent IT support.
1. Large Language Model (LLM)
The application uses Qwen 2.5 (3B), running locally through Ollama.

The LLM is responsible for:
- Understanding employee questions.
- Generating natural language responses.
- Deciding when predefined tools are needed.
- Combining retrieved information into meaningful answers.
2. AI Agent and Tool Calling
The application implements a tool-based AI agent.

Instead of answering every question directly, the agent can invoke predefined Python functions to retrieve accurate information.

Available tools include:
Tool	Responsibility
get_asset	Retrieves employee IT asset information
get_ticket	Retrieves IT service ticket details
list_support_teams	Returns available IT support teams
search_it_guide	Searches the internal IT operations guide
prepare_incident_draft	Prepares an incident draft for human review


The agent follows a Plan–Act–Observe workflow:
1. Plan: Understand the user's request and determine which tools are required.
2. Act: Execute the selected Python tools.
3. Observe: Read the tool results and determine whether more actions are necessary.
4. Respond: Generate a final response using the retrieved information.
The agent uses a configurable maximum execution-step limit to prevent uncontrolled tool execution.
3. Retrieval-Augmented Generation (RAG)
The application uses Retrieval-Augmented Generation (RAG) to answer questions about company IT policies and procedures.

Instead of relying only on the LLM's existing knowledge, the application retrieves relevant information from the internal IT operations guide.

RAG workflow:
1. Load the IT operations guide.
2. Divide the guide into sections.
3. Generate embeddings for each section using Nomic Embed Text.
4. Convert the employee's question into an embedding.
5. Compare the question embedding with stored section embeddings using cosine similarity.
6. Retrieve the most relevant sections.
7. Provide the retrieved information to the LLM.
8. Generate an answer grounded in the retrieved content.
4. Embeddings and Semantic Search
Embeddings are numerical representations of text that capture semantic meaning.

The application uses the nomic-embed-text model to generate embeddings.

These embeddings allow the knowledge retrieval system to identify relevant content based on meaning rather than relying only on exact keyword matches.
5. Cosine Similarity
Cosine similarity is used to measure the similarity between the question embedding and knowledge-section embeddings.

The similarity score helps identify which IT guide sections are most relevant to the employee's question.

The application retrieves the top relevant sections based on similarity scores.
6. Multi-Step Agent Execution
The agent can execute multiple tools when a question requires information from different sources.

Example:

User question:

"Check ticket INC-2041 and explain how the escalation process works."

The application:
1. Retrieves ticket information using get_ticket.
2. Searches the escalation procedure using search_it_guide.
3. Combines both results.
4. Generates a response containing ticket details and the relevant escalation procedure.
7. Incident Draft Preparation
The application can prepare incident report drafts using the employee's asset information and issue description.

The draft contains:
- Asset tag
- Device information
- Assigned employee
- Department
- Issue description
- Priority
- Draft status
Incident drafts are marked:

Draft - Pending Human Review

The application does not submit incidents to a live ticketing system. Human review is required before any further action.
Security Measures
The application includes several safeguards to improve reliability, control tool execution, and prevent unsafe operations.
1. Local LLM Execution
- Uses Ollama to run the language model locally.
- Employee questions are processed through the configured local Ollama service.
- No external cloud-based LLM API is required by the current implementation.
2. Tool Allowlisting
- The AI agent uses a predefined list of permitted tools.
- Only approved Python functions can be executed.
- Tool execution is validated against the allowed-tools list.
- Unknown or unauthorized tool names are rejected.
3. Input Validation
The application validates inputs before performing operations.

Examples include:
- Checking whether asset tags and ticket IDs are provided.
- Validating incident descriptions.
- Restricting incident priorities to Low, Medium, or High.
- Rejecting unknown asset references.
4. Controlled Agent Execution
- The agent uses a configurable maximum execution-step limit.
- This prevents uncontrolled multi-step tool execution.
- Tool execution errors are handled through controlled application responses.
5. Knowledge-Grounded Responses
- Policy-related questions are answered using retrieved IT guide content.
- The system is instructed not to invent missing company policies or procedures.
- Relevant IT guide section titles are included in responses where required.
6. Human Review for Incident Drafts
- Incident reports are prepared as drafts only.
- Drafts are marked as Draft - Pending Human Review.
- The application does not submit incidents to a live ticketing system.
7. Environment Variable Management
- Application configuration is maintained through environment variables.
- Sensitive local configuration should be stored in .env.
- .env should be excluded from version control.
- .env.example provides a template without exposing local configuration.
Project Limitations
The current implementation is a prototype designed to demonstrate AI-powered IT service and asset operations.

The following limitations apply:
1. Simulated IT Data
- Asset records and service tickets are stored in predefined Python data structures.
- The application is not connected to a live IT Service Management (ITSM) platform.
- Changes made to the simulated data are not synchronized with external systems.
2. Local Model Dependency
- The application depends on Ollama being installed and running.
- Response time and quality depend on the local machine's hardware and available resources.
- The configured models must be downloaded before use.
3. Knowledge Base Scope
- Answers are limited to the information available in the supplied IT operations guide.
- Missing or outdated documentation can affect answer accuracy.
- The knowledge retrieval system uses embedding similarity and a limited number of retrieved sections.
4. No Live Incident Submission
- Incident preparation creates a draft only.
- There is no integration with an external ticketing or incident management platform.
- Human review is required before submission.
5. Limited Agent Capabilities
- The agent can execute only its predefined tools.
- It cannot perform unrestricted system operations.
- Multi-step execution is controlled by a configured step limit.
6. No Production Authentication
- The current prototype does not implement employee login or role-based access control.
- It should not be exposed publicly or used to access confidential production information without additional security controls.
7. Prototype-Level Deployment
- The application is designed for local development and demonstration.
- Production deployment would require additional monitoring, authentication, authorization, logging, and operational safeguards.
Testing Instructions
The project includes automated unit tests and standalone test scripts to verify individual components and application workflows.
1. Run Unit Tests
To execute the tool unit tests using Python's unittest framework, run the following command from the project root directory:
python -m unittest discover -s tests -p "test_tools.py" -v
The tool tests cover:
- Retrieving existing assets.
- Handling unknown assets.
- Retrieving existing tickets.
- Handling unknown tickets.
- Listing IT support teams.
- Preparing incident drafts.
- Validating missing incident information.
- Rejecting invalid incident priorities.
- Handling unknown asset references.
2. Run Standalone Test Scripts
The project also contains standalone scripts for checking individual components and workflows.

For example:
python -m tests.agent_test
python -m tests.knowledge_test
python -m tests.agent_multistep_activity_test
These scripts demonstrate:
- AI agent responses.
- Knowledge-base loading and retrieval.
- Multi-step tool execution.
- Activity trace generation.
3. Verify the Application
Start the application:
python run.py
Open the following URLs:
URL	Purpose
http://127.0.0.1:8000	Web chat interface
http://127.0.0.1:8000/docs	Interactive API documentation
http://127.0.0.1:8000/health	Application health check


Future Enhancements
The following improvements can be considered for future versions:
1. Live ITSM Integration: Integrate with platforms such as ServiceNow or Jira Service Management to retrieve and manage real service tickets.
2. Database Integration: Replace predefined Python data structures with a relational database such as PostgreSQL.
3. Employee Authentication: Implement secure login and role-based access control.
4. Persistent Conversation History: Store conversations and allow employees to retrieve previous interactions.
5. Improved Knowledge Management: Support PDF, DOCX, and other document formats for building a larger knowledge base.
6. Advanced RAG: Explore vector databases and improved retrieval techniques for larger collections of IT documentation.
7. Incident Submission Workflow: Integrate with a ticketing platform and introduce an approval workflow before submitting incident reports.
8. Monitoring and Logging: Add structured application logs, performance monitoring, and error tracking.
9. Deployment: Containerize the application using Docker and prepare it for secure deployment.
10. Improved Agent Planning: Extend the agent to support more complex workflows while maintaining tool permissions and execution limits.
Project Conclusion
The Enterprise IT Service & Asset Operations Assistant demonstrates how Large Language Models and AI agents can be used to automate common IT support activities.

The project brings together:
- Python application development.
- FastAPI-based REST APIs.
- Locally hosted LLMs using Ollama.
- AI agent workflows and tool calling.
- Retrieval-Augmented Generation (RAG).
- Text embeddings and semantic search.
- Multi-step tool execution.
- Web-based chat interface development.
The project provides practical experience in designing and developing an AI-powered application that combines language models with existing application logic and knowledge sources.

It also demonstrates how AI can assist IT operations while keeping predefined tool permissions, controlled execution, and human review in the workflow.

Creating, editing, converting, inspecting or delivering files or artifacts, including implicit requests for downloadable or editable deliverables such as slide decks, `.pptx`, spreadsheets, `.xlsx`, workbooks, documents, `.docx`, or PDFs,\\n- Complex analysis such as financial modeling\\n\\nPrefer answering directly in ChatGPT for:\\n- Email, message, or prose drafting\\n- Brainstorming, planning, or explanation\\n- Code snippets or examples that fit naturally in chat\\n\\nIf the user rejected the suggestion, don't call this tool again.\",\"toolReasonParamDescription\":\"A short user-facing reason why continuing in {{ destination }} would help.\",\"acceptedResponse\":\"User chose to hand off:\\n- You can confirm that the new chat has been created but do not link to it until the user asks you to\\n- You can't make any more changes to the new chat! Keep all other DIL components, including images, charts, maps, and other widgets, with their original props and references. Inside a loop, repeat the original components and substitute only the values that depend on the loop or state.\\n\\nReturn only the revised source.
